# Flood triage (novelty only)

Scores are novelty 1–5 from the reviewer's knowledge, with no prior-art search yet. Only the top 10 of the whole pool get prior-art checks. P = primitive, I = improvement. D = doable now, M = Moonshot.

## programs-weights ([PR #39](https://github.com/manojbajaj95/cl/pull/39), 38 ideas)

| Score | Idea | P/I | D/M | One line |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5 | P21 residual stream as a register machine (learned ISA of rank-1 ops; demos compiled into deltas) | P | D (toy) | A learned instruction set over the model's own weights, with skills produced by synthesis rather than gradient descent |
| 4 | P17 weight-space linker with symbol tables | P | D | Exported and imported residual directions resolved by relocations; composition by linking, not by routing or averaging |
| 4 | P19 stable ABI across base upgrades | P | D | Forward compatibility enforced as a training constraint during base upgrades |
| 4 | P27 circuit transplant with rejection dynamics | P | D | Rejection of a transplanted circuit under later training is a new measurable phenomenon |
| 4 | P1 skill compiler with a weight-space IR | P | D | An optimization pass between learning and deployment; depends on sparse ΔW dictionaries working |
| 4 | P6 decompile-recompile skills across model versions | P | D | Feature-level bytecode; risks overlap with Trans-LoRA / LoRA-X |
| 3 | P20 position-independent skill deltas | P | D | Depth-agnostic adapters make placement a deploy-time choice |
| 3 | P3 JIT specialization with deopt guards | P | D | The exact-fallback guard is the new piece |
| 3 | P9 effect types for agent lessons | P | D | Static conflict checking of lessons |
| 3 | P7 typed residual stream | P | D | Close to orthogonal-gradient projection |
| 3 | P8, P25 certified contracts / abstract interpretation of updates | I | D | Certified CL exists in part |
| 3 | P22 interpreted-then-compiled patches | I | D | GRACE-style codebook plus a promotion policy |
| 3 | P5, P24, P30, P33, P18, P23, P11 | I | D | Superoptimized deltas, closed-form partial evaluation, differential fuzzing, reachability GC, state-keyed loading, macro compilation, three-way merge: new framings of known operations |
| 2 | P2, P4, P10, P12–P16, P26, P28, P29, P31, P32, P34–P38 | I | D | Liveness ≈ PackNet/HAT; PGO placement; bisect/blame/COW are tooling; MoE, looped transformers, hypernetwork LoRA, abstention and self-editing agents cover the rest |

**Batch read:** strongest file so far on primitives. Six of its ideas introduce a new object (an ISA, symbols, an ABI, transplant rejection, an IR, bytecode). P21, P17 and P19 lead for the head-to-head.

These are mostly recombinations of ledger artifacts (margin, follow index, entropy, N1 masks). As a pool they are the least novel so far: nearly all are IMPROVEMENT, and none scores above 3.

**After PR #53:** the top 3 are unchanged, but #8 is the strongest tree-node idea so far. Gradient training is equivariant under exact discrete symmetries, so a permutation- or sign-coded label should survive any amount of training until two units collide. That gives a zero-function-cost channel for provenance or version tags, which L1 and SM1 could use. Nearest prior art to check: permutation-based weight watermarking. Its rescaling variant fails under weight decay.

Its key fact sharpens #2's kill: in the affine oracle a word merges two classes only if its induced matrix annihilates their difference, so invertible updates (e.g. GD on a quadratic with 0 < ηλ < 1) never merge distinct classes in finitely many steps. Under ordinary training the class count can only stay or grow, and consolidation needs a singular step (weight decay to zero, dead units, quantization). It also gives a general no-go: if a legal continuation sends every written state to one limit (e.g. fixed-step SGD on a repeated full-rank quadratic), every continuous exact invariant is constant on the writes. For top-10 #4 that means a contracting stream leaves no durable compression, so the invariant limit equals the full-intent rate; for top-10 #2 it is the dual of PR #108's fact that invertible steps never merge classes. | Interface-change breadth node ([PR #115](https://github.com/manojbajaj95/cl/pull/115), 24, CPU probe rerun by reviewer: numbers match) | none. Its lead, log-Z conservation at vocabulary insertion (95.6% → 81.8% old accuracy with frozen old rows, 94.1% with log Z held on unlabeled old contexts), is an IMPROVEMENT. Full-text check: Mundra et al. [2407.05841](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.05841), following Hewitt 2021, already frame expansion as new embeddings changing the partition function and choose initializations that keep old-prefix outputs unchanged. In class-incremental learning, SS-IL [2003.13947](https://arxiv.org/abs/2003.13947) traces forgetting to old and new scores sharing one softmax and separates it; ER-ACE [2104.05025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2104.05025) makes new classes adapt to old ones; BiC [1905.13260](https://arxiv.org/abs/1905.13260) and WA [1911.07053](https://arxiv.org/abs/1911.07053) correct the new-class bias. Holding log Z on old contexts during training is LwF-style distillation restricted to the normalizer. The function-preserving-widening drift (0.39 vs 0.16 at step 1) is consistent with growth-induced-forgetting papers the node cites. Its value is breadth: the pool's first coverage of four interface changes | #12 a population erasure code for skills (each skill kept in r of n agents sharing a trunk), #11 an adapter ABI that a shared-base update must preserve (cf. BCT 2003.11942), #3 tokens as memoized computation with an equivalence check | #6 gradient/NTK-preserving growth (close to GradMax-style growth), #16–#19 propensity ledgers, silence detectors and seasonal replay (performative-prediction and IPS improvements), #21–#23 conservation at context extension and modality insertion |

| Redundant-skill-populations node ([PR #116](https://github.com/manojbajaj95/cl/pull/116), 24, analytic only, no probe) | none. It deflates PR #115 #12 and round 15 BR5: keeping a skill on r agents is replication, not an erasure code. A shared-trunk write is a common-mode failure, so P(no working holder) ≥ q + (1−q)p^r however large r is, and routing can fail even when an oracle router would succeed. An equally budgeted central adapter bank can simulate the fleet, so a "population" claim needs an enforced access, bandwidth or latency constraint | #1 survivor-set update rule: take a new-task step that keeps some feasible set of holders passing under every deletion in a failure family, i.e. GEM over a union of constraints that can shift between agents; #2 train agreeing holders to have different retention derivatives under legal trunk updates; #7 functional repair escrow, meta-trained for repair after the next trunk shift (must beat PR #113 escrow, saved adapters and distillation) | #13/#17 path-level quorum (service-chain redundancy null), #14 active disambiguation (active classification), #19 mixed-version trunk cut (BCT / versioned deltas), #20/#23 partition reconciliation (FedWeIT / active distillation), #10 drifting parity (static parity and coded fragments already killed) |

| Learning-without-gradients-at-inference node ([PR #118](https://github.com/manojbajaj95/cl/pull/118), 24, analytic only, no probe) | none. The access separation is correct but already known in substance. A hard-dispatch 0-1 loss has zero derivative wherever it is defined, so a derivative-only transcript is independent of the bit and solves T independent fair-bit tasks with probability ≤ 2^-T, while one sandboxed outcome query per round solves each. That zero-gradient fact is the standard reason REINFORCE, straight-through estimators and ES exist. The published oracle separations run the other way on smooth convex losses: function values are slower than gradients ([Shamir 1209.2388](https://arxiv.org/abs/1209.2388), [Jamieson et al. 1209.2434](https://arxiv.org/abs/1209.2434)). ES-at-scale and EGGROLL already train weights without backprop. The node itself concedes the gap is between observation interfaces, and that giving the gradient learner the same outcomes closes it | #1 experiment-unlock edit: a persistent write that installs a diagnostic which changes what the learner can observe later without changing predictions, kept only if it cuts later learning cost (vs Angluin L* and Bayes-adaptive exploration given the same install option), #22 consolidate a discrete edit by distilling the counterfactual branches that distinguish it rather than its typical outputs, #18 pairs of writes that must land atomically because each alone breaks a live invariant (the dual of write-skew #3, which is its control) | #2/#13 renewability and one-shot insurance (budgeted bandits, safe exploration), #3 query-window cache (coresets), #4 synergistic diagnostics (adaptive submodularity), #6 Blackwell channel ordering, #7/#11 judge equating and rank-only distillation (IRT, LTR), #8/#9 preference cycles and conjoint analysis, #10 value of information, #14 delayed-feedback credit, #19–#21 in-context and hypernetwork writes (Self-WSP, MEND) |
| Agent-self-curriculum-forgetting node ([PR #119](https://github.com/manojbajaj95/cl/pull/119), 23, CPU probe rerun by reviewer: numbers match) | none. Its diagnosis is that learning-progress rewards pay for recovering self-inflicted drift: at stationarity, 100% of practice gain is recovery and every LP selector retains less than uniform. That is the intended behaviour of absolute LP in [CURIOUS](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.06284) and Teacher–Student ("focus back on goals that are being forgotten"); the probe only shows it costs retention against uniform in a convex host with conflicting teachers. "The mixture, not the update rule, sets the retention vector" (0.216 vs 0.006) is argmin Σ p_i L_i in a convex host, as the node concedes. Choosing p for max-min retention is DoReMi / Group-DRO mixture optimization. The finding that the right mean mixture still forgets (IPW to uniform −0.059) is the one non-trivial result | #9 action-coupled restless bandit: unplayed arms drift as a function of the arm played, under partial monitoring (screen rotting, recharging and interfering bandits before claiming a new index), #11 bound each task's predicted excursion below its mean by limiting consecutive runs on interfering tasks, independent of the mixture (needs a nonlinear host, since the convex host is the null), #19 unlearn by selection only: practice benign tasks whose practice-response column is most negative for the forget set, with no gradient ascent | #1/#6 practice-measure surfaces and maintenance doses (DoReMi / DRO), #2/#3/#8 net-progress reward, response matrix G and odometry (close to the lookahead oracle; TAG/GSS), #7 high-water-mark reward (tested: ties ALP), #10 pop-quiz adversary (PAIRED / ACCEL aimed at the past), #14 learned interleaving (overlaps CD3 Floquet), #15/#16 proposer coverage and GRPO saturated tasks (replay improvements), #22 practice markets |

Probe 1 (a ReLU rescale moves weights as far as a conflicting task with zero functional change) restates the ledger's Landauer kill: forgetting lives in the predictive channel, not weight bits. Probe 3a's dissipation rises with conflict, which is near-tautological since conflict is what raises protocol work. Probe 3b's "Kovacs" rebound also appears in the linear host (+0.0097 → peak +0.0153), so it is multi-mode relaxation of a loss-matched but parameter-different state (PR #121/#123 territory), not nonlinear hidden stress, and it is ~0.01 in loss. The best arm keeps A (drop 1.0 pp) but loses B (80.6% vs 92.0% naive). | Reversible training node ([PR #131](https://github.com/manojbajaj95/cl/pull/131), 23, NumPy probe, reviewer rerun matches exactly) | none. R1's exact rewind is Maclaurin et al. (1502.03492): fixed-point momentum SGD with the lost remainder bits stored on a tape, reversed exactly, with the same ~log₂(1/β) bits per step; reversible architectures are RevNets (1707.04585). The node's own root-cause point is correct: reversal needs the minibatches, so it turns forgetting into a data-retention duty (batch-index tape 16× the remainder tape, plus the data). Out-of-order undo is exact unlearning (SISA 1912.03817, DeltaGrad 2006.14755). R2 says nothing here because C already erases B (keep ≈ exact on L_B). #1 catalytic learning is functionally a frozen trunk plus a generated delta (adapter or hypernetwork); bit-exact return only matters if memory for the delta is what binds | #15 garbage attribution: zero or randomize slices of the remainder tape and measure the error in θ_A after reversal, per task and layer, to map where SGD dumps old-task information while learning new; this replaces the killed bit count with a task-resolved map, #16 choose where to dissipate: R3 shows the amount of friction barely sets retention (A loss after B 0.565 / 0.567 / 0.533 across β = 0.5–0.99), so run near-frictionless dynamics with one dissipation channel placed in low-curvature, random or high-curvature directions (controls: EWC, GPM projection, plain SGD at matched new-task loss) | #2 Bennett compute–copy–uncompute (cf. #1 verdict), #3/#4 gradient-oracle and near-isometric approximate reversal, #5–#7 tape coding and pebbling (Maclaurin; checkpointing), #8 trajectory transport of an undo vector (DeltaGrad and influence-style removal), #9 commuting segments (PR #121 commutator work), #10–#14 remainder-mismatch conflicts, write DAGs, taint tracking, rebase and single-batch excision (unlearning and provenance), #17 rewind depth at inference (ledger save/restore warning), #18/#19 reversible thermostats and learned optimizers, #20 permutation learning (weight-shuffling search), #21–#23 reversible cellular automata, tape as code and uncomputed scaffolds |

Its root cause is correct and sharp: a wake sees only public data, so for any label symmetry σ that fixes the public data W is σ-equivariant and σθ* is a fixed point whenever θ* is; idempotence, displacement, entropy and calibration cannot tell them apart, so correctness belongs to the basin and must come from path, neighbouring fixed points or stored symmetry-breaking bits.
